From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n10\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.0, F51.0, Z72.5\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, it’s... I guess it all really started when I had to move into this new place a little while back. Though calling it a place is a joke, honestly, it’s just this totally busted-up room. I came in today because ever since I got in there, everything’s just going off the rails. \n\nLike, the ceiling’s got this leak right over where I’m supposed to sleep, and the radiator is either dead cold or banging like a jackhammer. So I’m up half the night, every single night, trying to patch up leaks with whatever scrap I can find or messing with the heat just so I don't freeze to death. And because I’m up doing all that, I’m just... I’m completely dragging myself to the job site the next morning. I do construction, mostly labor and framing, and you really can’t be half-asleep doing that. But I'm rolling in late, which my foreman is already on my case about, and I'm messing up basic stuff I normally handle in my sleep. Like, measuring wrong, dropping materials, just stupid mistakes. \n\nAnd on top of it, the guys on the crew will say one little thing to me, just regular joking around, and I’m just snapping at them. Like, really biting their heads off over absolutely nothing. I ain't normally an angry guy, you know? I usually get along with everybody. But ever since I got stuck in that room, it’s just one thing after another, and I just can't seem to get my head straight to do my job or even just talk to people without losing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: Anxious? Nah, I wouldn't... I don't think that's the right word for it. When I hear anxious, I think of, you know, somebody sitting around biting their fingernails off, worrying about what's gonna happen tomorrow or if the sky is falling or whatever. That ain't really my deal. I ain't sitting in that room shaking with nerves or nothing. \n\nIt's more just... I'm just so damn tired, man. And when you're that tired, your fuse is just gone. Like, zero fuse left. Just the other day I was hauling this stack of two-by-fours, and my arms felt like pure lead because I only got maybe, I don't know, two hours of sleep? Because that radiator was doing that clank, clank, clank thing again all night long. So it ain't that I'm on edge like I'm scared or panicking about stuff. It's just that my body is completely giving out on me, and I'm just so frustrated with the whole setup. \n\nI mean, anybody would be, right? You try sleeping with a pipe screaming in your ear and a draft freezing your toes off, and then tell me if you feel anxious or if you just want to punch a wall because you can't get five minutes of peace. So no, I wouldn't say I'm anxious. Just worn down to the bone. Just totally tapped out.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: Sad? No, I wouldn't say sad. I mean, sure, it sucks being in that room, but I'm not sitting around crying about it or feeling sorry for myself. I ain't depressed or nothing like that. It's more just... anger, you know? And frustration. \n\nLike I was saying before, it's just this constant grind of not sleeping, going to work exhausted, messing up, getting yelled at, and then coming back to that same freezing, leaky room to do it all over again. It's like I'm stuck on this miserable hamster wheel, and I can't figure out how to jump off. \n\nI guess maybe sometimes, when I'm lying there at 3 AM staring at the water stain on the ceiling, listening to that radiator bang, I might feel a little... defeated? Like, man, how did I end up in this dump? But it's not a sad feeling. It's more like a heavy feeling. Like I'm just dragging around a hundred-pound sack of bricks all day. \n\nBut no, not sad. I don't get teary-eyed or feel like life is hopeless or anything. I just want a decent night's sleep and for my foreman to get off my back. If I could just fix those two things, I'd be golden. But right now, it just feels like I'm running on empty, and everybody's expecting me to perform like I've got a full tank. And you just can't do that, especially not in construction. You need your strength, you need your focus, and I just don't have it right now. So yeah, not sad, just... totally worn out and fed up with the whole situation.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: My appetite? Nah, not really. I mean, I guess I eat about the same as I always do. It's not like I'm starving myself or anything, and I'm definitely not stress-eating either. \n\nHonestly, with the way things are going, I barely have time to even think about food, let alone sit down and have a proper meal. When you're working construction, you gotta eat just to keep the engine running, you know? So I still grab my usual stuff—like, I'll stop at this deli near the site and get a bacon, egg, and cheese on a roll in the morning, and maybe a hero or some pizza for lunch. I gotta have the calories because I'm burning so much energy hauling lumber and swinging hammers all day. \n\nBut it's not like I'm enjoying it or anything. It's just fuel. I'll be sitting on a bucket during my break, shoving a sandwich in my face, and all I can think about is how tired I am and how much my back hurts from sleeping on that lumpy mattress in that freezing room. And then when I get home... well, I call it home, but you know what I mean... I'm usually too exhausted to even bother cooking. Not that I could cook much anyway, since the stove in that place is half busted too. Only two of the burners work, and one of them smells like gas if you leave it on for more than five minutes, so I don't even mess with it. \n\nSo yeah, I'll just grab some takeout on the way back, maybe Chinese or a burger, and eat it sitting on the edge of the bed while I stare at the wall and listen to the radiator clank. So I'm still eating, I'm still putting the food away, but the appetite itself hasn't changed. It's just... eating is just another chore now, you know? Just another thing I gotta do to get through the day so I don't pass out on the job site. But the amount I'm eating, nah, that's pretty much exactly the same as it was before I moved into that dump.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: Relationships? With friends or family? Nah, I wouldn't say there's been any big changes or nothing. I mean, my family, they're all back out in Ohio, so it's not like I see them much anyway. We talk on the phone maybe once every couple of weeks, usually on a Sunday. My mom calls and asks if I'm eating right and if I'm staying out of trouble, the usual mom stuff. And I just tell her yeah, everything's fine, work is good. I don't tell her about the room or the radiator or the fact that I'm barely sleeping, because, you know, what's the point? She'd just worry, and there ain't nothing she can do about it from three states away. So I just keep it brief with her. \n\nAnd as for my friends... well, the guys I hang out with are mostly the guys from the site. We used to hit up this bar down the street from the job after we'd clock out on Fridays, grab a few beers, shoot some pool, just unwind after busting our humps all week. And I guess... well, I guess I haven't really been doing that as much lately. But it ain't because we got beef or nothing! We're still cool. We still joke around on the site, you know, when I'm not too tired to crack a smile. \n\nIt's just that when Friday rolls around now, all I can think about is getting back to that room and trying to pass out before the weekend noise starts up. Because on the weekends, the guy in the apartment above me likes to blast this heavy bass music, and it vibrates right through the ceiling. So if I don't get to sleep before he starts his little party, I'm screwed. So yeah, the guys will be like, \"Hey man, you coming for a pint?\" and I'll just make some excuse about having to do laundry or run errands. I just don't have the energy to stand around a loud bar and pretend I'm having a good time when my eyes are practically burning out of my skull from being so tired. \n\nBut like I said, it's not like we're not friends anymore. They get it, mostly. Construction guys know what it's like to be beat. They probably just think I'm picking up extra shifts or something. I just... I just don't have it in me to be social right now. You know how it is when you're just running on fumes? You barely have enough gas to get yourself through the day, let alone be the life of the party. So I just keep to myself a bit more, try to conserve what little energy I got left. But nah, no real changes. Just... just too tired to hang out, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable more often than usual since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: Irritable? Man, \"irritable\" is putting it mildly. I mean... yeah. I am. I'm snapping at everybody. Honestly, that's a big part of why I even came in here to talk to somebody, because ever since I moved into that busted-up room, my fuse is just completely gone. I got zero patience left for anything or anyone. \n\nAnd how could I not be? It's like I was saying, I'm spending half the night just trying to make the place livable. Like the other night, the radiator started hissing and spitting water everywhere, so I'm up at 2 AM with a wrench trying to mess with the valve just so my work boots don't get soaked. And then the ceiling leak starts dripping right onto the foot of the mattress. So I'm staying up fixing leaks, messing with the heat, moving buckets around... just doing whatever I can to get a few hours of peace. \n\nBy the time the sun comes up, I'm completely wrecked. So then I'm dragging myself late to work, which is a huge deal on a site. You can't be rolling in after the whistle. The foreman is already on my case for that, and then... and this is the part that really gets under my skin, because I know my job, I'm a good worker... but I'm messing up stuff I normally handle in my sleep. I'm measuring cuts wrong, I'm dropping materials, I'm forgetting instructions two seconds after the boss gives them to me. Just stupid, stupid rookie mistakes because my brain is just total mush.\n\nAnd when you're messing up, and the foreman is yelling, and you're running on two hours of sleep... yeah, I snap. I snapped at one of the guys, Tommy, just yesterday. He just asked if I had a spare tape measure, a totally normal question, and I just bit his head off. Yelled at him to get his own damn tools. Poor guy didn't do nothing wrong, and I felt bad about it later, but in the moment, I just couldn't help it. It's like I'm a rubber band pulled way too tight, and every little thing just makes me snap. So yeah, I'm definitely more irritable. It's just bleeding into everything—my work, how I treat the guys, everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: Trouble sleeping? I mean... yeah, Doc, like I was just saying with the radiator and the leaks and all that. It's... it's impossible. I feel like I just gave you the whole rundown, but man, it's honestly the biggest problem I got right now. I'm barely sleeping at all. \n\nI'll get home, right? And I'm already dead on my feet from hauling materials all day. I'll eat my takeout on the edge of the bed, and then I'll try to lay down. But the mattress that came with this room—I swear it's older than I am. It's got this one busted spring that just jams right into my ribs. If I roll over, it gets my hip. If I lay flat, it's right in my spine. So I'm tossing and turning for like an hour just trying to find a spot that doesn't bruise me. \n\nAnd then, just when my eyes start getting heavy, the noises start. If it's not the guy upstairs rattling my ceiling with his bass music, it's the pipes. Have you ever heard an old steam radiator when the valve is messed up? It sounds like somebody is down in the walls just wailing on the pipes with a metal wrench. Clank, clank, clank. And it's not a steady beat either, so you can't even get used to it. You're just lying there in the dark, waiting for the next bang. \n\nPlus, the window in there doesn't shut flush, so there's this freezing draft blowing right across my face all night. I got like three cheap blankets piled on top of me, I'm wearing my work hoodie to bed, and I'm still shivering. So between freezing, getting stabbed by a spring, waiting for the radiator to explode, and getting up to move buckets around when the ceiling drips... I'm lucky if I string together two or three hours of actual sleep. By the time my alarm goes off at 5 AM, my eyes feel like they're full of sand. So yeah, trouble sleeping is putting it pretty lightly. I don't even know what a real night of sleep feels like anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel irritable even on the rare occasions when you do get a decent night's sleep?\n\nPatient: Irritable when I actually sleep? Nah, man. No way. I mean, the problem is those nights are like finding a needle in a haystack right now, you know? But... well, take two weekends ago, for example. By some absolute miracle, the guy upstairs wasn't home. Must've gone out of town or stayed at his girlfriend's place or something, because there was no heavy bass, no stomping around on the floorboards. And the radiator, for whatever reason, decided to give me a break and didn't start clanking and spitting all night. I actually slept. I think I got a solid seven, maybe even eight hours. \n\nAnd man, let me tell you, when I woke up that Sunday? I felt like a completely different person. I felt like myself again. I wasn't mad at nobody. I even went down to the corner store to grab a coffee, and the guy behind the counter, he completely messed up my order. Put in like three sugars when I always take it black. Now, if that happened yesterday? I probably would've lost my mind on the poor guy over a cup of coffee. But that morning, because I actually slept? I just laughed it off. I was like, hey, no worries, I'll drink it sweet today, it's all good. \n\nSo, no, if I get my rest, I'm just regular old me. I'm a pretty easy-going guy usually! You could ask any of the guys on the crew from before I moved into this dump. I'm usually the one cracking jokes, keeping things light when we're pouring concrete in the hot sun and everybody else is miserable. I don't like being angry. It feels awful, honestly, walking around with your fists clenched all the time. It's just... my tank is completely empty right now. If I could just get a normal night's sleep on a regular basis, where I don't have to sleep in my work hoodie or dodge a busted mattress spring, I wouldn't be snapping at Tommy or the foreman or nobody else. It's purely the exhaustion, Doc. I swear, if I just had a quiet room and a decent bed, I'd be totally fine. I wouldn't even be sitting here talking to you right now, no offense. It's just the tiredness making me so short-tempered.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel irritable if your living situation and sleep improved?\n\nPatient: Honestly? No. I really don't think I would be. Like I just said, if I get my sleep, I'm good. I'm golden. But... I mean, the thing you gotta understand is that this whole housing mess? It hasn't really ended yet. Not even close. I'm still living right in the thick of it. \n\nI'm still in that same busted room. Every single day I clock out of work, I gotta go back there and deal with the exact same crap. I'm still doing the repairs myself because the landlord... man, don't even get me started on this guy. I call him, I leave voicemails—like, \"Hey, the ceiling is leaking right onto where I sleep,\" or \"Hey, the radiator is spitting boiling water\"—and he just ghosts me. Completely ignores me. Or if he does pick up, he says he's \"sending a guy Tuesday,\" and then Tuesday comes and goes and nobody shows up. So I'm still the one up at 2 AM with a roll of duct tape and a wrench trying to hold the place together. \n\nSo, you know, it's not like... it's not like this is some situation that got fixed a month ago, and I'm just sitting around holding a grudge and staying mad long after it was over, you know? It's not like the problem is gone and my brain is just stuck being angry for no reason. It's happening right now. Tonight, I gotta go back to that same exact room, and I'm gonna have to dodge that same mattress spring, and I'll probably have to text that same useless landlord again just to get ignored. \n\nIf all that actually got resolved? If the place got fixed up, or if I could somehow afford to break the lease and get a decent apartment where things actually work? Man, the irritability would be gone in a second. It would vanish. Because the stress would be gone, and I'd actually be sleeping. But right now? I'm still in the middle of the fire, Doc. So yeah, until the living situation actually improves and I'm not playing handyman for a slumlord all night, I'm probably gonna keep having a short fuse.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your irritability would completely go away if you were able to get consistent, good-quality sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, Doc, like I was just saying a minute ago, absolutely. If I could get consistent, good sleep, I'd be totally back to normal. I'd be my regular self. But the thing is—and I feel like I gotta keep coming back to this so you get the full picture of what I'm dealing with—my sleep is tied directly to that room. \n\nAnd this whole housing mess? It hasn't really ended yet. I mean, it's not even close to being resolved. I'm still in that exact same room right this second. I'm still dealing with the repairs every single night after I get off a ten-hour shift hauling materials. I’m still fighting with the landlord who won't even text me back about a leaking ceiling or a busted radiator. I'm the one buying the duct tape and the buckets and trying to keep the walls from rotting out. \n\nSo, you know, it's not like this is something that just kept going long after it was over. It's not like the guy fixed the place three months ago, or I moved into a nice new apartment, and I'm just sitting around holding a grudge and staying mad for no reason. If the situation was actually over and done with, and I was still walking around wanting to bite people's heads off, then yeah, maybe I'd think my brain was just stuck on angry. But the stress is happening right now. I'm still living in it every single day. \n\nSo if you're asking if the irritability would completely go away with good sleep, yeah, 100 percent, it would. But I can't get that consistent sleep until this whole landlord and repair nightmare is actually in the rearview mirror, you know what I mean? Because right now, I'm still right in the thick of it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean... no, not really. Look, I know we keep going in circles about this, but you gotta understand, the whole reason I even came in to see you in the first place—the only reason I'm sitting in this chair right now—is because ever since I moved into that busted-up room, everything just completely fell apart. 

I'm not a naturally angry dude, I swear. I don't just walk around looking for things to be mad at. But now? I'm staying up all hours of the night trying to fix leaks with buckets and whatever I can find, or I'm banging on the pipes trying to get the heat to actually work so I don't freeze to death in my own bed. And then, because I'm doing all that handyman garbage instead of sleeping, I end up snapping at people for no good reason. 

And it's bleeding into my job now, which is the real problem, honestly. That's what really pushed me to come talk to somebody. I'm dragging myself in late to work because I finally pass out at like 4 AM, and then my alarm goes off at 5:30, so I end up oversleeping. The foreman is already chewing me out for that. And then when I'm actually on the clock, I'm messing up stuff I normally handle in my sleep. I've been on a work site since I was a teenager, man. I know what I'm doing. But the other day I completely botched a simple measurement, cut a board totally wrong, just 'cause my brain is completely fried from dealing with this apartment. 

So, no, if it wasn't for this room, I wouldn't be irritable at all. I wouldn't be messing up at work, I wouldn't be late, and I wouldn't be snapping at the guys. It all just traces right back to that busted-up room and the stress of trying to keep a roof over my head that isn't actively caving in.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, it’s... I guess it all really started when I had to move into this new place a little while back. Though calling it a place is a joke, honestly, it’s just this totally busted-up room. I came in today because ever since I got in there, everything’s just going off the rails. \n\nLike, the ceiling’s got this leak right over where I’m supposed to sleep, and the radiator is either dead cold or banging like a jackhammer. So I’m up half the night, every single night, trying to patch up leaks with whatever scrap I can find or messing with the heat just so I don't freeze to death. And because I’m up doing all that, I’m just... I’m completely dragging myself to the job site the next morning. I do construction, mostly labor and framing, and you really can’t be half-asleep doing that. But I'm rolling in late, which my foreman is already on my case about, and I'm messing up basic stuff I normally handle in my sleep. Like, measuring wrong, dropping materials, just stupid mistakes. \n\nAnd on top of it, the guys on the crew will say one little thing to me, just regular joking around, and I’m just snapping at them. Like, really biting their heads off over absolutely nothing. I ain't normally an angry guy, you know? I usually get along with everybody. But ever since I got stuck in that room, it’s just one thing after another, and I just can't seem to get my head straight to do my job or even just talk to people without losing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: Anxious? Nah, I wouldn't... I don't think that's the right word for it. When I hear anxious, I think of, you know, somebody sitting around biting their fingernails off, worrying about what's gonna happen tomorrow or if the sky is falling or whatever. That ain't really my deal. I ain't sitting in that room shaking with nerves or nothing. \n\nIt's more just... I'm just so damn tired, man. And when you're that tired, your fuse is just gone. Like, zero fuse left. Just the other day I was hauling this stack of two-by-fours, and my arms felt like pure lead because I only got maybe, I don't know, two hours of sleep? Because that radiator was doing that clank, clank, clank thing again all night long. So it ain't that I'm on edge like I'm scared or panicking about stuff. It's just that my body is completely giving out on me, and I'm just so frustrated with the whole setup. \n\nI mean, anybody would be, right? You try sleeping with a pipe screaming in your ear and a draft freezing your toes off, and then tell me if you feel anxious or if you just want to punch a wall because you can't get five minutes of peace. So no, I wouldn't say I'm anxious. Just worn down to the bone. Just totally tapped out.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: Sad? No, I wouldn't say sad. I mean, sure, it sucks being in that room, but I'm not sitting around crying about it or feeling sorry for myself. I ain't depressed or nothing like that. It's more just... anger, you know? And frustration. \n\nLike I was saying before, it's just this constant grind of not sleeping, going to work exhausted, messing up, getting yelled at, and then coming back to that same freezing, leaky room to do it all over again. It's like I'm stuck on this miserable hamster wheel, and I can't figure out how to jump off. \n\nI guess maybe sometimes, when I'm lying there at 3 AM staring at the water stain on the ceiling, listening to that radiator bang, I might feel a little... defeated? Like, man, how did I end up in this dump? But it's not a sad feeling. It's more like a heavy feeling. Like I'm just dragging around a hundred-pound sack of bricks all day. \n\nBut no, not sad. I don't get teary-eyed or feel like life is hopeless or anything. I just want a decent night's sleep and for my foreman to get off my back. If I could just fix those two things, I'd be golden. But right now, it just feels like I'm running on empty, and everybody's expecting me to perform like I've got a full tank. And you just can't do that, especially not in construction. You need your strength, you need your focus, and I just don't have it right now. So yeah, not sad, just... totally worn out and fed up with the whole situation.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: My appetite? Nah, not really. I mean, I guess I eat about the same as I always do. It's not like I'm starving myself or anything, and I'm definitely not stress-eating either. \n\nHonestly, with the way things are going, I barely have time to even think about food, let alone sit down and have a proper meal. When you're working construction, you gotta eat just to keep the engine running, you know? So I still grab my usual stuff—like, I'll stop at this deli near the site and get a bacon, egg, and cheese on a roll in the morning, and maybe a hero or some pizza for lunch. I gotta have the calories because I'm burning so much energy hauling lumber and swinging hammers all day. \n\nBut it's not like I'm enjoying it or anything. It's just fuel. I'll be sitting on a bucket during my break, shoving a sandwich in my face, and all I can think about is how tired I am and how much my back hurts from sleeping on that lumpy mattress in that freezing room. And then when I get home... well, I call it home, but you know what I mean... I'm usually too exhausted to even bother cooking. Not that I could cook much anyway, since the stove in that place is half busted too. Only two of the burners work, and one of them smells like gas if you leave it on for more than five minutes, so I don't even mess with it. \n\nSo yeah, I'll just grab some takeout on the way back, maybe Chinese or a burger, and eat it sitting on the edge of the bed while I stare at the wall and listen to the radiator clank. So I'm still eating, I'm still putting the food away, but the appetite itself hasn't changed. It's just... eating is just another chore now, you know? Just another thing I gotta do to get through the day so I don't pass out on the job site. But the amount I'm eating, nah, that's pretty much exactly the same as it was before I moved into that dump.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: Relationships? With friends or family? Nah, I wouldn't say there's been any big changes or nothing. I mean, my family, they're all back out in Ohio, so it's not like I see them much anyway. We talk on the phone maybe once every couple of weeks, usually on a Sunday. My mom calls and asks if I'm eating right and if I'm staying out of trouble, the usual mom stuff. And I just tell her yeah, everything's fine, work is good. I don't tell her about the room or the radiator or the fact that I'm barely sleeping, because, you know, what's the point? She'd just worry, and there ain't nothing she can do about it from three states away. So I just keep it brief with her. \n\nAnd as for my friends... well, the guys I hang out with are mostly the guys from the site. We used to hit up this bar down the street from the job after we'd clock out on Fridays, grab a few beers, shoot some pool, just unwind after busting our humps all week. And I guess... well, I guess I haven't really been doing that as much lately. But it ain't because we got beef or nothing! We're still cool. We still joke around on the site, you know, when I'm not too tired to crack a smile. \n\nIt's just that when Friday rolls around now, all I can think about is getting back to that room and trying to pass out before the weekend noise starts up. Because on the weekends, the guy in the apartment above me likes to blast this heavy bass music, and it vibrates right through the ceiling. So if I don't get to sleep before he starts his little party, I'm screwed. So yeah, the guys will be like, \"Hey man, you coming for a pint?\" and I'll just make some excuse about having to do laundry or run errands. I just don't have the energy to stand around a loud bar and pretend I'm having a good time when my eyes are practically burning out of my skull from being so tired. \n\nBut like I said, it's not like we're not friends anymore. They get it, mostly. Construction guys know what it's like to be beat. They probably just think I'm picking up extra shifts or something. I just... I just don't have it in me to be social right now. You know how it is when you're just running on fumes? You barely have enough gas to get yourself through the day, let alone be the life of the party. So I just keep to myself a bit more, try to conserve what little energy I got left. But nah, no real changes. Just... just too tired to hang out, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable more often than usual since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: Irritable? Man, \"irritable\" is putting it mildly. I mean... yeah. I am. I'm snapping at everybody. Honestly, that's a big part of why I even came in here to talk to somebody, because ever since I moved into that busted-up room, my fuse is just completely gone. I got zero patience left for anything or anyone. \n\nAnd how could I not be? It's like I was saying, I'm spending half the night just trying to make the place livable. Like the other night, the radiator started hissing and spitting water everywhere, so I'm up at 2 AM with a wrench trying to mess with the valve just so my work boots don't get soaked. And then the ceiling leak starts dripping right onto the foot of the mattress. So I'm staying up fixing leaks, messing with the heat, moving buckets around... just doing whatever I can to get a few hours of peace. \n\nBy the time the sun comes up, I'm completely wrecked. So then I'm dragging myself late to work, which is a huge deal on a site. You can't be rolling in after the whistle. The foreman is already on my case for that, and then... and this is the part that really gets under my skin, because I know my job, I'm a good worker... but I'm messing up stuff I normally handle in my sleep. I'm measuring cuts wrong, I'm dropping materials, I'm forgetting instructions two seconds after the boss gives them to me. Just stupid, stupid rookie mistakes because my brain is just total mush.\n\nAnd when you're messing up, and the foreman is yelling, and you're running on two hours of sleep... yeah, I snap. I snapped at one of the guys, Tommy, just yesterday. He just asked if I had a spare tape measure, a totally normal question, and I just bit his head off. Yelled at him to get his own damn tools. Poor guy didn't do nothing wrong, and I felt bad about it later, but in the moment, I just couldn't help it. It's like I'm a rubber band pulled way too tight, and every little thing just makes me snap. So yeah, I'm definitely more irritable. It's just bleeding into everything—my work, how I treat the guys, everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: Trouble sleeping? I mean... yeah, Doc, like I was just saying with the radiator and the leaks and all that. It's... it's impossible. I feel like I just gave you the whole rundown, but man, it's honestly the biggest problem I got right now. I'm barely sleeping at all. \n\nI'll get home, right? And I'm already dead on my feet from hauling materials all day. I'll eat my takeout on the edge of the bed, and then I'll try to lay down. But the mattress that came with this room—I swear it's older than I am. It's got this one busted spring that just jams right into my ribs. If I roll over, it gets my hip. If I lay flat, it's right in my spine. So I'm tossing and turning for like an hour just trying to find a spot that doesn't bruise me. \n\nAnd then, just when my eyes start getting heavy, the noises start. If it's not the guy upstairs rattling my ceiling with his bass music, it's the pipes. Have you ever heard an old steam radiator when the valve is messed up? It sounds like somebody is down in the walls just wailing on the pipes with a metal wrench. Clank, clank, clank. And it's not a steady beat either, so you can't even get used to it. You're just lying there in the dark, waiting for the next bang. \n\nPlus, the window in there doesn't shut flush, so there's this freezing draft blowing right across my face all night. I got like three cheap blankets piled on top of me, I'm wearing my work hoodie to bed, and I'm still shivering. So between freezing, getting stabbed by a spring, waiting for the radiator to explode, and getting up to move buckets around when the ceiling drips... I'm lucky if I string together two or three hours of actual sleep. By the time my alarm goes off at 5 AM, my eyes feel like they're full of sand. So yeah, trouble sleeping is putting it pretty lightly. I don't even know what a real night of sleep feels like anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel irritable even on the rare occasions when you do get a decent night's sleep?\n\nPatient: Irritable when I actually sleep? Nah, man. No way. I mean, the problem is those nights are like finding a needle in a haystack right now, you know? But... well, take two weekends ago, for example. By some absolute miracle, the guy upstairs wasn't home. Must've gone out of town or stayed at his girlfriend's place or something, because there was no heavy bass, no stomping around on the floorboards. And the radiator, for whatever reason, decided to give me a break and didn't start clanking and spitting all night. I actually slept. I think I got a solid seven, maybe even eight hours. \n\nAnd man, let me tell you, when I woke up that Sunday? I felt like a completely different person. I felt like myself again. I wasn't mad at nobody. I even went down to the corner store to grab a coffee, and the guy behind the counter, he completely messed up my order. Put in like three sugars when I always take it black. Now, if that happened yesterday? I probably would've lost my mind on the poor guy over a cup of coffee. But that morning, because I actually slept? I just laughed it off. I was like, hey, no worries, I'll drink it sweet today, it's all good. \n\nSo, no, if I get my rest, I'm just regular old me. I'm a pretty easy-going guy usually! You could ask any of the guys on the crew from before I moved into this dump. I'm usually the one cracking jokes, keeping things light when we're pouring concrete in the hot sun and everybody else is miserable. I don't like being angry. It feels awful, honestly, walking around with your fists clenched all the time. It's just... my tank is completely empty right now. If I could just get a normal night's sleep on a regular basis, where I don't have to sleep in my work hoodie or dodge a busted mattress spring, I wouldn't be snapping at Tommy or the foreman or nobody else. It's purely the exhaustion, Doc. I swear, if I just had a quiet room and a decent bed, I'd be totally fine. I wouldn't even be sitting here talking to you right now, no offense. It's just the tiredness making me so short-tempered.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel irritable if your living situation and sleep improved?\n\nPatient: Honestly? No. I really don't think I would be. Like I just said, if I get my sleep, I'm good. I'm golden. But... I mean, the thing you gotta understand is that this whole housing mess? It hasn't really ended yet. Not even close. I'm still living right in the thick of it. \n\nI'm still in that same busted room. Every single day I clock out of work, I gotta go back there and deal with the exact same crap. I'm still doing the repairs myself because the landlord... man, don't even get me started on this guy. I call him, I leave voicemails—like, \"Hey, the ceiling is leaking right onto where I sleep,\" or \"Hey, the radiator is spitting boiling water\"—and he just ghosts me. Completely ignores me. Or if he does pick up, he says he's \"sending a guy Tuesday,\" and then Tuesday comes and goes and nobody shows up. So I'm still the one up at 2 AM with a roll of duct tape and a wrench trying to hold the place together. \n\nSo, you know, it's not like... it's not like this is some situation that got fixed a month ago, and I'm just sitting around holding a grudge and staying mad long after it was over, you know? It's not like the problem is gone and my brain is just stuck being angry for no reason. It's happening right now. Tonight, I gotta go back to that same exact room, and I'm gonna have to dodge that same mattress spring, and I'll probably have to text that same useless landlord again just to get ignored. \n\nIf all that actually got resolved? If the place got fixed up, or if I could somehow afford to break the lease and get a decent apartment where things actually work? Man, the irritability would be gone in a second. It would vanish. Because the stress would be gone, and I'd actually be sleeping. But right now? I'm still in the middle of the fire, Doc. So yeah, until the living situation actually improves and I'm not playing handyman for a slumlord all night, I'm probably gonna keep having a short fuse.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your irritability would completely go away if you were able to get consistent, good-quality sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, Doc, like I was just saying a minute ago, absolutely. If I could get consistent, good sleep, I'd be totally back to normal. I'd be my regular self. But the thing is—and I feel like I gotta keep coming back to this so you get the full picture of what I'm dealing with—my sleep is tied directly to that room. \n\nAnd this whole housing mess? It hasn't really ended yet. I mean, it's not even close to being resolved. I'm still in that exact same room right this second. I'm still dealing with the repairs every single night after I get off a ten-hour shift hauling materials. I’m still fighting with the landlord who won't even text me back about a leaking ceiling or a busted radiator. I'm the one buying the duct tape and the buckets and trying to keep the walls from rotting out. \n\nSo, you know, it's not like this is something that just kept going long after it was over. It's not like the guy fixed the place three months ago, or I moved into a nice new apartment, and I'm just sitting around holding a grudge and staying mad for no reason. If the situation was actually over and done with, and I was still walking around wanting to bite people's heads off, then yeah, maybe I'd think my brain was just stuck on angry. But the stress is happening right now. I'm still living in it every single day. \n\nSo if you're asking if the irritability would completely go away with good sleep, yeah, 100 percent, it would. But I can't get that consistent sleep until this whole landlord and repair nightmare is actually in the rearview mirror, you know what I mean? Because right now, I'm still right in the thick of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would ever feel irritable for reasons other than lack of sleep or your current living situation?\n\nPatient: I mean... no, not really. Look, I know we keep going in circles about this, but you gotta understand, the whole reason I even came in to see you in the first place—the only reason I'm sitting in this chair right now—is because ever since I moved into that busted-up room, everything just completely fell apart. \n\nI'm not a naturally angry dude, I swear. I don't just walk around looking for things to be mad at. But now? I'm staying up all hours of the night trying to fix leaks with buckets and whatever I can find, or I'm banging on the pipes trying to get the heat to actually work so I don't freeze to death in my own bed. I know what I'm doing. It all just traces right back to that busted-up room and the stress of trying to keep a roof over my head that isn't actively caving in.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I was like, hey, no worries, I'll drink it sweet today, it's all good. \n\nSo, no, if I get my rest, I'm just regular old me. I'm a pretty easy-going guy usually! You could ask any of the guys on the crew from before I moved into this dump. I'm usually the one cracking jokes, keeping things light when we're pouring concrete in the hot sun and everybody else is miserable. I don't like being angry. It feels awful, honestly, walking around with your fists clenched all the time. It's just... my tank is completely empty right now. If I could just get a normal night's sleep on a regular basis, where I don't have to sleep in my work hoodie or dodge a busted mattress spring, I wouldn't be snapping at Tommy or the foreman or nobody else. It's purely the exhaustion, Doc. I swear, if I just had a quiet room and a decent bed, I'd be totally fine. I wouldn't even be sitting here talking to you right now, no offense. It's just the tiredness making me so short-tempered.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel irritable if your living situation and sleep improved?\n\nPatient: Honestly? No. I really don't think I would be. Like I just said, if I get my sleep, I'm good. I'm golden. But... I mean, the thing you gotta understand is that this whole housing mess? It hasn't really ended yet. Not even close. I'm still living right in the thick of it. \n\nI'm still in that same busted room. Every single day I clock out of work, I gotta go back there and deal with the exact same crap. I'm still doing the repairs myself because the landlord... man, don't even get me started on this guy. I call him, I leave voicemails—like, \"Hey, the ceiling is leaking right onto where I sleep,\" or \"Hey, the radiator is spitting boiling water\"—and he just ghosts me. Completely ignores me. Or if he does pick up, he says he's \"sending a guy Tuesday,\" and then Tuesday comes and goes and nobody shows up. So I'm still the one up at 2 AM with a roll of duct tape and a wrench trying to hold the place together. \n\nSo, you know, it's not like... it's not like this is some situation that got fixed a month ago, and I'm just sitting around holding a grudge and staying mad long after it was over, you know? It's not like the problem is gone and my brain is just stuck being angry for no reason. It's happening right now. Tonight, I gotta go back to that same exact room, and I'm gonna have to dodge that same mattress spring, and I'll probably have to text that same useless landlord again just to get ignored. \n\nIf all that actually got resolved? If the place got fixed up, or if I could somehow afford to break the lease and get a decent apartment where things actually work? Man, the irritability would be gone in a second. It would vanish. Because the stress would be gone, and I'd actually be sleeping. But right now? I'm still in the middle of the fire, Doc. So yeah, until the living situation actually improves and I'm not playing handyman for a slumlord all night, I'm probably gonna keep having a short fuse.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your irritability would completely go away if you were able to get consistent, good-quality sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, Doc, like I was just saying a minute ago, absolutely. If I could get consistent, good sleep, I'd be totally back to normal. I'd be my regular self. But the thing is—and I feel like I gotta keep coming back to this so you get the full picture of what I'm dealing with—my sleep is tied directly to that room. \n\nAnd this whole housing mess? It hasn't really ended yet. I mean, it's not even close to being resolved. I'm still in that exact same room right this second. I'm still dealing with the repairs every single night after I get off a ten-hour shift hauling materials. I’m still fighting with the landlord who won't even text me back about a leaking ceiling or a busted radiator. I'm the one buying the duct tape and the buckets and trying to keep the walls from rotting out. \n\nSo, you know, it's not like this is something that just kept going long after it was over. It's not like the guy fixed the place three months ago, or I moved into a nice new apartment, and I'm just sitting around holding a grudge and staying mad for no reason. If the situation was actually over and done with, and I was still walking around wanting to bite people's heads off, then yeah, maybe I'd think my brain was just stuck on angry. But the stress is happening right now. I'm still living in it every single day. \n\nSo if you're asking if the irritability would completely go away with good sleep, yeah, 100 percent, it would. But I can't get that consistent sleep until this whole landlord and repair nightmare is actually in the rearview mirror, you know what I mean? Because right now, I'm still right in the thick of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would ever feel irritable for reasons other than lack of sleep or your current living situation?\n\nPatient: I mean... no, not really. Look, I know we keep going in circles about this, but you gotta understand, the whole reason I even came in to see you in the first place—the only reason I'm sitting in this chair right now—is because ever since I moved into that busted-up room, everything just completely fell apart. \n\nI'm not a naturally angry dude, I swear. I don't just walk around looking for things to be mad at. But now? I'm staying up all hours of the night trying to fix leaks with buckets and whatever I can find, or I'm banging on the pipes trying to get the heat to actually work so I don't freeze to death in my own bed. And then, because I'm doing all that handyman garbage instead of sleeping, I end up snapping at people for no good reason. \n\nAnd it's bleeding into my job now, which is the real problem, honestly. That's what really pushed me to come talk to somebody. I'm dragging myself in late to work because I finally pass out at like 4 AM, and then my alarm goes off at 5:30, so I end up oversleeping. I know what I'm doing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Doc, I gotta be honest, I feel like you're messing with me a little bit here because we keep circling back to the exact same question. I don't mean to be disrespectful or nothing, but... no, man. No. It wouldn't persist. If the place was fixed, I'd be fine. 

I feel like maybe I'm just not explaining it right, or maybe you're looking for some deep psychological reason for why I'm ticked off all the time, but it's really just basic math. The housing mess hasn't really ended yet. It's still happening right this very second. I'm still in that exact same room. When I leave your office today, I gotta go right back there and I'm still the one dealing with all the repairs, and I'm still the one fighting with that deadbeat landlord just to get basic living conditions. 

So, you know, this isn't something that just kept going long after it was over. If I had moved out six months ago, or if the landlord had actually stepped up and fixed the pipes and the heat back in November, and I was *still* sitting here snapping at you and messing up on the job site? Then yeah, I'd tell you, "Doc, something's wrong with my brain, the anger is persisting." But that's not what's happening. It's not lingering from the past. It's literally my life right now. 

If you put me in a normal apartment tonight where the roof doesn't leak and the heat actually turns on when it's freezing outside, the irritability would be gone by tomorrow morning. I'd wake up a completely different guy. But until then, I'm stuck in the middle of it. The mess hasn't ended, so the stress hasn't ended. It's really that simple.

Like, really biting their heads off over absolutely nothing. I ain't normally an angry guy, you know? I usually get along with everybody. But ever since I got stuck in that room, it’s just one thing after another, and I just can't seem to get my head straight to do my job or even just talk to people without losing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: Anxious? Nah, I wouldn't... I don't think that's the right word for it. That ain't really my deal. I ain't sitting in that room shaking with nerves or nothing. \n\nIt's more just... I'm just so damn tired, man. And when you're that tired, your fuse is just gone. Because that radiator was doing that clank, clank, clank thing again all night long. So it ain't that I'm on edge like I'm scared or panicking about stuff. It's just that my body is completely giving out on me, and I'm just so frustrated with the whole setup. \n\nI mean, anybody would be, right? No, I wouldn't say sad. I mean, sure, it sucks being in that room, but I'm not sitting around crying about it or feeling sorry for myself. I ain't depressed or nothing like that. It's more just... anger, you know? And frustration. \n\nLike I was saying before, it's just this constant grind of not sleeping, going to work exhausted, messing up, getting yelled at, and then coming back to that same freezing, leaky room to do it all over again. It's like I'm stuck on this miserable hamster wheel, and I can't figure out how to jump off. \n\nI guess maybe sometimes, when I'm lying there at 3 AM staring at the water stain on the ceiling, listening to that radiator bang, I might feel a little... defeated? Like, man, how did I end up in this dump? But it's not a sad feeling. It's more like a heavy feeling. Like I'm just dragging around a hundred-pound sack of bricks all day. \n\nBut no, not sad. I don't get teary-eyed or feel like life is hopeless or anything. I just want a decent night's sleep and for my foreman to get off my back. If I could just fix those two things, I'd be golden. But right now, it just feels like I'm running on empty, and everybody's expecting me to perform like I've got a full tank. And you just can't do that, especially not in construction. You need your strength, you need your focus, and I just don't have it right now. So yeah, not sad, just... totally worn out and fed up with the whole situation.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: My appetite? Nah, not really. I mean, I guess I eat about the same as I always do. It's not like I'm starving myself or anything, and I'm definitely not stress-eating either. \n\nHonestly, with the way things are going, I barely have time to even think about food, let alone sit down and have a proper meal. When you're working construction, you gotta eat just to keep the engine running, you know? So I still grab my usual stuff—like, I'll stop at this deli near the site and get a bacon, egg, and cheese on a roll in the morning, and maybe a hero or some pizza for lunch. I gotta have the calories because I'm burning so much energy hauling lumber and swinging hammers all day. \n\nBut it's not like I'm enjoying it or anything. It's just fuel. I'll be sitting on a bucket during my break, shoving a sandwich in my face, and all I can think about is how tired I am and how much my back hurts from sleeping on that lumpy mattress in that freezing room. And then when I get home... well, I call it home, but you know what I mean... I'm usually too exhausted to even bother cooking. Not that I could cook much anyway, since the stove in that place is half busted too. Only two of the burners work, and one of them smells like gas if you leave it on for more than five minutes, so I don't even mess with it. \n\nSo yeah, I'll just grab some takeout on the way back, maybe Chinese or a burger, and eat it sitting on the edge of the bed while I stare at the wall and listen to the radiator clank. So I'm still eating, I'm still putting the food away, but the appetite itself hasn't changed. It's just... eating is just another chore now, you know? Just another thing I gotta do to get through the day so I don't pass out on the job site. But the amount I'm eating, nah, that's pretty much exactly the same as it was before I moved into that dump.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: Relationships? With friends or family? Nah, I wouldn't say there's been any big changes or nothing. I mean, my family, they're all back out in Ohio, so it's not like I see them much anyway. We talk on the phone maybe once every couple of weeks, usually on a Sunday. My mom calls and asks if I'm eating right and if I'm staying out of trouble, the usual mom stuff. And I just tell her yeah, everything's fine, work is good. I don't tell her about the room or the radiator or the fact that I'm barely sleeping, because, you know, what's the point? She'd just worry, and there ain't nothing she can do about it from three states away. So I just keep it brief with her. \n\nAnd as for my friends... well, the guys I hang out with are mostly the guys from the site. We used to hit up this bar down the street from the job after we'd clock out on Fridays, grab a few beers, shoot some pool, just unwind after busting our humps all week. And I guess... well, I guess I haven't really been doing that as much lately. But it ain't because we got beef or nothing! We're still cool. We still joke around on the site, you know, when I'm not too tired to crack a smile. \n\nIt's just that when Friday rolls around now, all I can think about is getting back to that room and trying to pass out before the weekend noise starts up. Because on the weekends, the guy in the apartment above me likes to blast this heavy bass music, and it vibrates right through the ceiling. So if I don't get to sleep before he starts his little party, I'm screwed. So yeah, the guys will be like, \"Hey man, you coming for a pint?\" and I'll just make some excuse about having to do laundry or run errands. I just don't have the energy to stand around a loud bar and pretend I'm having a good time when my eyes are practically burning out of my skull from being so tired. \n\nBut like I said, it's not like we're not friends anymore. They get it, mostly. Construction guys know what it's like to be beat. They probably just think I'm picking up extra shifts or something. I just... I just don't have it in me to be social right now. You know how it is when you're just running on fumes? You barely have enough gas to get yourself through the day, let alone be the life of the party. So I just keep to myself a bit more, try to conserve what little energy I got left. But nah, no real changes. Just... just too tired to hang out, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable more often than usual since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: Irritable? Man, \"irritable\" is putting it mildly. I mean... yeah. I am. I'm snapping at everybody. Honestly, that's a big part of why I even came in here to talk to somebody, because ever since I moved into that busted-up room, my fuse is just completely gone. I got zero patience left for anything or anyone. \n\nAnd how could I not be? It's like I was saying, I'm spending half the night just trying to make the place livable. Like the other night, the radiator started hissing and spitting water everywhere, so I'm up at 2 AM with a wrench trying to mess with the valve just so my work boots don't get soaked. And then the ceiling leak starts dripping right onto the foot of the mattress. So then I'm dragging myself late to work, which is a huge deal on a site. You can't be rolling in after the whistle. The foreman is already on my case for that, and then... and this is the part that really gets under my skin, because I know my job, I'm a good worker... but I'm messing up stuff I normally handle in my sleep. I'm measuring cuts wrong, I'm dropping materials, I'm forgetting instructions two seconds after the boss gives them to me. Just stupid, stupid rookie mistakes because my brain is just total mush.\n\nAnd when you're messing up, and the foreman is yelling, and you're running on two hours of sleep... yeah, I snap. I snapped at one of the guys, Tommy, just yesterday. He just asked if I had a spare tape measure, a totally normal question, and I just bit his head off. Yelled at him to get his own damn tools. Poor guy didn't do nothing wrong, and I felt bad about it later, but in the moment, I just couldn't help it. It's like I'm a rubber band pulled way too tight, and every little thing just makes me snap. So yeah, I'm definitely more irritable. It's just bleeding into everything—my work, how I treat the guys, everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: Trouble sleeping? If I lay flat, it's right in my spine. It sounds like somebody is down in the walls just wailing on the pipes with a metal wrench. Clank, clank, clank. And it's not a steady beat either, so you can't even get used to it. You're just lying there in the dark, waiting for the next bang. \n\nPlus, the window in there doesn't shut flush, so there's this freezing draft blowing right across my face all night. I got like three cheap blankets piled on top of me, I'm wearing my work hoodie to bed, and I'm still shivering. Now, if that happened yesterday? I probably would've lost my mind on the poor guy over a cup of coffee. But that morning, because I actually slept? I just laughed it off. I was like, hey, no worries, I'll drink it sweet today, it's all good. \n\nSo, no, if I get my rest, I'm just regular old me. I'm a pretty easy-going guy usually! You could ask any of the guys on the crew from before I moved into this dump. I'm usually the one cracking jokes, keeping things light when we're pouring concrete in the hot sun and everybody else is miserable. I don't like being angry. It feels awful, honestly, walking around with your fists clenched all the time. It's just... my tank is completely empty right now. If I could just get a normal night's sleep on a regular basis, where I don't have to sleep in my work hoodie or dodge a busted mattress spring, I wouldn't be snapping at Tommy or the foreman or nobody else. It's just the tiredness making me so short-tempered.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel irritable if your living situation and sleep improved?\n\nPatient: Honestly? I snapped at one of the guys, Tommy, just yesterday. He just asked if I had a spare tape measure, a totally normal question, and I just bit his head off. Yelled at him to get his own damn tools. Poor guy didn't do nothing wrong, and I felt bad about it later, but in the moment, I just couldn't help it. It's like I'm a rubber band pulled way too tight, and every little thing just makes me snap. So yeah, I'm definitely more irritable. It's just bleeding into everything—my work, how I treat the guys, everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: Trouble sleeping? I mean... yeah, Doc, like I was just saying with the radiator and the leaks and all that. It's... it's impossible. I feel like I just gave you the whole rundown, but man, it's honestly the biggest problem I got right now. I'm barely sleeping at all. \n\nI'll get home, right? And I'm already dead on my feet from hauling materials all day. I'll eat my takeout on the edge of the bed, and then I'll try to lay down. But the mattress that came with this room—I swear it's older than I am. It's got this one busted spring that just jams right into my ribs. If I roll over, it gets my hip. If I lay flat, it's right in my spine. So I'm tossing and turning for like an hour just trying to find a spot that doesn't bruise me. \n\nAnd then, just when my eyes start getting heavy, the noises start. If it's not the guy upstairs rattling my ceiling with his bass music, it's the pipes. Have you ever heard an old steam radiator when the valve is messed up? It sounds like somebody is down in the walls just wailing on the pipes with a metal wrench. Clank, clank, clank. And it's not a steady beat either, so you can't even get used to it. You're just lying there in the dark, waiting for the next bang. \n\nPlus, the window in there doesn't shut flush, so there's this freezing draft blowing right across my face all night. I got like three cheap blankets piled on top of me, I'm wearing my work hoodie to bed, and I'm still shivering. So between freezing, getting stabbed by a spring, waiting for the radiator to explode, and getting up to move buckets around when the ceiling drips... I'm lucky if I string together two or three hours of actual sleep. By the time my alarm goes off at 5 AM, my eyes feel like they're full of sand. So yeah, trouble sleeping is putting it pretty lightly. I don't even know what a real night of sleep feels like anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel irritable even on the rare occasions when you do get a decent night's sleep?\n\nPatient: Irritable when I actually sleep? Nah, man. No way. I mean, the problem is those nights are like finding a needle in a haystack right now, you know? But... well, take two weekends ago, for example. By some absolute miracle, the guy upstairs wasn't home. Must've gone out of town or stayed at his girlfriend's place or something, because there was no heavy bass, no stomping around on the floorboards. And the radiator, for whatever reason, decided to give me a break and didn't start clanking and spitting all night. I actually slept. I think I got a solid seven, maybe even eight hours. \n\nAnd man, let me tell you, when I woke up that Sunday? I felt like a completely different person. I felt like myself again. I wasn't mad at nobody. I even went down to the corner store to grab a coffee, and the guy behind the counter, he completely messed up my order. Put in like three sugars when I always take it black. Now, if that happened yesterday? I probably would've lost my mind on the poor guy over a cup of coffee. But that morning, because I actually slept? I just laughed it off. I was like, hey, no worries, I'll drink it sweet today, it's all good. \n\nSo, no, if I get my rest, I'm just regular old me. I'm a pretty easy-going guy usually! You could ask any of the guys on the crew from before I moved into this dump. I'm usually the one cracking jokes, keeping things light when we're pouring concrete in the hot sun and everybody else is miserable. I don't like being angry. It feels awful, honestly, walking around with your fists clenched all the time. It's just... my tank is completely empty right now. If I could just get a normal night's sleep on a regular basis, where I don't have to sleep in my work hoodie or dodge a busted mattress spring, I wouldn't be snapping at Tommy or the foreman or nobody else. It's purely the exhaustion, Doc. I swear, if I just had a quiet room and a decent bed, I'd be totally fine. I wouldn't even be sitting here talking to you right now, no offense. It's just the tiredness making me so short-tempered.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel irritable if your living situation and sleep improved?\n\nPatient: Honestly? No. I really don't think I would be. Like I just said, if I get my sleep, I'm good. I'm golden. But... I mean, the thing you gotta understand is that this whole housing mess? It hasn't really ended yet. Not even close. I'm still living right in the thick of it. \n\nI'm still in that same busted room. Every single day I clock out of work, I gotta go back there and deal with the exact same crap. I'm still doing the repairs myself because the landlord... man, don't even get me started on this guy. I call him, I leave voicemails—like, \"Hey, the ceiling is leaking right onto where I sleep,\" or \"Hey, the radiator is spitting boiling water\"—and he just ghosts me. Completely ignores me. Or if he does pick up, he says he's \"sending a guy Tuesday,\" and then Tuesday comes and goes and nobody shows up. So I'm still the one up at 2 AM with a roll of duct tape and a wrench trying to hold the place together. \n\nSo, you know, it's not like... it's not like this is some situation that got fixed a month ago, and I'm just sitting around holding a grudge and staying mad long after it was over, you know? It's not like the problem is gone and my brain is just stuck being angry for no reason. It's happening right now. Tonight, I gotta go back to that same exact room, and I'm gonna have to dodge that same mattress spring, and I'll probably have to text that same useless landlord again just to get ignored. \n\nIf all that actually got resolved? If the place got fixed up, or if I could somehow afford to break the lease and get a decent apartment where things actually work? Man, the irritability would be gone in a second. It would vanish. Because the stress would be gone, and I'd actually be sleeping. But right now? I'm still in the middle of the fire, Doc. So yeah, until the living situation actually improves and I'm not playing handyman for a slumlord all night, I'm probably gonna keep having a short fuse.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your irritability would completely go away if you were able to get consistent, good-quality sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, Doc, like I was just saying a minute ago, absolutely. If I could get consistent, good sleep, I'd be totally back to normal. I'd be my regular self. But the thing is—and I feel like I gotta keep coming back to this so you get the full picture of what I'm dealing with—my sleep is tied directly to that room. \n\nAnd this whole housing mess? It hasn't really ended yet. I mean, it's not even close to being resolved. I'm still in that exact same room right this second. I'm still dealing with the repairs every single night after I get off a ten-hour shift hauling materials. I’m still fighting with the landlord who won't even text me back about a leaking ceiling or a busted radiator. I'm the one buying the duct tape and the buckets and trying to keep the walls from rotting out. \n\nSo, you know, it's not like this is something that just kept going long after it was over. It's not like the guy fixed the place three months ago, or I moved into a nice new apartment, and I'm just sitting around holding a grudge and staying mad for no reason. If the situation was actually over and done with, and I was still walking around wanting to bite people's heads off, then yeah, maybe I'd think my brain was just stuck on angry. But the stress is happening right now. I'm still living in it every single day. \n\nSo if you're asking if the irritability would completely go away with good sleep, yeah, 100 percent, it would. But I can't get that consistent sleep until this whole landlord and repair nightmare is actually in the rearview mirror, you know what I mean? Because right now, I'm still right in the thick of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would ever feel irritable for reasons other than lack of sleep or your current living situation?\n\nPatient: I mean... no, not really. Look, I know we keep going in circles about this, but you gotta understand, the whole reason I even came in to see you in the first place—the only reason I'm sitting in this chair right now—is because ever since I moved into that busted-up room, everything just completely fell apart. \n\nI'm not a naturally angry dude, I swear. I don't just walk around looking for things to be mad at. But now? I'm staying up all hours of the night trying to fix leaks with buckets and whatever I can find, or I'm banging on the pipes trying to get the heat to actually work so I don't freeze to death in my own bed. And then, because I'm doing all that handyman garbage instead of sleeping, I end up snapping at people for no good reason. \n\nAnd it's bleeding into my job now, which is the real problem, honestly. That's what really pushed me to come talk to somebody. I'm dragging myself in late to work because I finally pass out at like 4 AM, and then my alarm goes off at 5:30, so I end up oversleeping. The foreman is already chewing me out for that. And then when I'm actually on the clock, I'm messing up stuff I normally handle in my sleep. I've been on a work site since I was a teenager, man. I know what I'm doing. But the other day I completely botched a simple measurement, cut a board totally wrong, just 'cause my brain is completely fried from dealing with this apartment. \n\nSo, no, if it wasn't for this room, I wouldn't be irritable at all. I wouldn't be messing up at work, I wouldn't be late, and I wouldn't be snapping at the guys. It all just traces right back to that busted-up room and the stress of trying to keep a roof over my head that isn't actively caving in.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your irritability would persist even if your sleep and living situation were to significantly improve?\n\nPatient: Doc, I gotta be honest, I feel like you're messing with me a little bit here because we keep circling back to the exact same question. I don't mean to be disrespectful or nothing, but... no, man. No. It wouldn't persist. If the place was fixed, I'd be fine. \n\nI feel like maybe I'm just not explaining it right, or maybe you're looking for some deep psychological reason for why I'm ticked off all the time, but it's really just basic math. The housing mess hasn't really ended yet. It's still happening right this very second. I'm still in that exact same room. When I leave your office today, I gotta go right back there and I'm still the one dealing with all the repairs, and I'm still the one fighting with that deadbeat landlord just to get basic living conditions. \n\nSo, you know, this isn't something that just kept going long after it was over. If I had moved out six months ago, or if the landlord had actually stepped up and fixed the pipes and the heat back in November, and I was *still* sitting here snapping at you and messing up on the job site? Then yeah, I'd tell you, \"Doc, something's wrong with my brain, the anger is persisting.\" But that's not what's happening. It's not lingering from the past. It's literally my life right now. \n\nIf you put me in a normal apartment tonight where the roof doesn't leak and the heat actually turns on when it's freezing outside, the irritability would be gone by tomorrow morning. I'd wake up a completely different guy. But until then, I'm stuck in the middle of it. The mess hasn't ended, so the stress hasn't ended. It's really that simple.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean, sure, everyone gets a little ticked off sometimes, right? Like if somebody cuts you off in traffic or you drop a hammer on your thumb—stuff like that. But nothing like this. Not this kind of constant, grinding irritability where I feel like my skin is too tight and I'm just waiting for someone to look at me wrong. 

Honestly, before this whole housing nightmare, I was doing pretty normal. I was just a regular guy going to work, hanging out with my buddies on the weekends, maybe catching a game or grabbing a couple beers. I didn't have this black cloud hanging over me all the time. I wasn't snapping at the guys on the crew or getting written up for being late. Life was just... life, you know? It was fine. 

But then I moved into that room. And I swear to you, Doc, it was like flipping a switch. I mean, the first few days were okay, but this all started within the first couple weeks of finding the water damage and the heat problems. I remember the exact night, actually. It was raining sideways, and I woke up because water was literally dripping onto my forehead. My forehead! In my own bed! And then I realized the radiator was ice cold. That was the start of it. That was the moment everything just went downhill. So yeah, before that room, I was fine. It's just... it's all tied to that place, man. I'm telling you, it's that room.
